Reviewed the durable photo staging queue after the save/rollback checks. Metadata lookup, native fetch, full-body buffering and base64 encoding occurred before the existing serialized write/rename section. Concurrent saves could therefore buffer several complete inputs while waiting for commits. A queued operation could also inspect metadata before its later account-revision check. Two regressions reproduced these behaviors; the read-failure/next-job control passed.

Local staging now enters the shared filesystem queue before resolving/inspecting/reading input. The queue holds through metadata validation, body validation, encoding, idempotency comparison, atomic temporary-file commit and verification. The original captured revision is checked when queued work begins, so stale work fails before metadata/body access. Valid remote/canonical references still take their no-read fast path. Existing ownership, size, encoding, unchanged-byte and cleanup checks remain intact. A read rejection is propagated while later queue operations remain usable. The change does not serialize native image decoding or Storage uploads and is not a whole-app memory cap.

All 61 focused staging/thumbnail/item tests pass; full Jest passes 1,410 tests / 123 suites; TypeScript passes; targeted lint has zero errors/warnings. Three added cases check delayed second buffering/FIFO commit, stale queued metadata denial and continued work after read rejection. The previous-source run had two failures and 23 passes in the service suite; all 25 service cases now pass.

Extended the existing actual-source OS-filesystem adapter/restart harness with eight simultaneous 2,915,128-byte PNG staging requests. A controlled five-ms body delay exposes overlap; peak active body reads was one, all eight files committed with exact hashes (23,321,024 bytes), source bytes remained unchanged and batch files were removed. Existing 64-MiB sparse-file preflight still rejects before body read; picker eviction, container rebasing and idempotent recovery through a fresh Node process pass. All temporary files were removed. This uses Node with an Expo-shaped filesystem adapter, not on-device Expo execution or RSS profiling. [OS adapter result](readiness-evidence/staging-buffer-os-durability.json), [regressions/source hashes](readiness-evidence/staging-buffer-serialization/verification.json).

Android normal Release APK rebuilt successfully. Current normal-entry iOS JavaScript was repackaged in the unchanged compiled native runtime; both source maps match the staging service and omit audit entries. [Artifact checks](readiness-evidence/staging-buffer-serialization/normal-artifacts.json). No simulator, cloud user/object or provider operation was created in this turn. Native RSS/high-resolution decoder memory, caller-owned queued data URI strings and upload buffers remain outside this proof. A pending local read now holds the filesystem queue until it settles; no new native-read timeout was added. Device memory/I/O-duration profiling, full UI and overall 10,000 DAU capacity/security/store/provider acceptance remain open. The recovery test-inbox request is still pending; AI-provider work remains deferred.

The local image reader now rejects response statuses 300 and above before reading the body, preventing error/redirect bodies with image signatures from being uploaded. Status 0 remains supported for local adapters. Nine regression cases failed before the fix; all 52 focused tests and 1,422 tests across 123 suites now pass. TypeScript, targeted lint and the OS-file durability test pass. Normal Android release compilation and iOS normal-entry Hermes export completed; both maps contain the exact current media and recovery sources. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/local-image-response-status/`. These checks do not establish native fetch-status behavior, full image decoding, native RSS bounds, I/O deadlines or 10,000-DAU readiness.


### 2026-10-18 — Live recovery delivery blocked by unverified Resend domain

One normal iOS recovery request for a tagged disposable, confirmed account returned the UI error “Error sending recovery email.” The matching production Auth log at 09:09 UTC records POST `/recover` HTTP 500 (`unexpected_failure`), with SMTP 550: the `aiwardrobe.club` domain is not verified in Resend. This establishes a production recovery-email blocker; inbox delivery and recovery-link routing remain unverified. No resend was attempted. The disposable account was deleted after exact owner/tag checks; independent SQL confirms zero users, sessions, identities and profiles for its UUID. The dedicated simulator was removed, leaving the existing simulator untouched. Resend requires sign-in before domain configuration can be inspected. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/recovery-live-delivery-20261018.json`.


### 2026-10-18 — Process authentication links before route publication

Replaced the independent App auth URL listener with NavigationContainer custom `getInitialURL` and `subscribe` handlers. Cold and warm navigation now receive each link after its auth operation settles, preventing the reset screen from binding to a previous account while the recovery exchange is pending. Warm events preserve arrival order; removed subscribers cannot navigate or start queued work; thrown startup work returns no route. The existing store catches some SDK errors internally, so this does not establish that every settled operation succeeded. Eight added deterministic tests include cold/warm rendered reset-form binding to the recovered owner, delayed processing, event order, unsubscribe and thrown-work behavior. All 1,430 tests / 124 suites and TypeScript pass; lint reports zero errors and one existing native-screens require warning. Normal Android release build and iOS Hermes export pass with exact current App/helper/reset-screen sources in their maps. No live recovery redirect has been verified because Resend domain verification remains blocked. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/auth-linking-order/`.


### 2026-10-18 — Reject reset forms without a successfully processed recovery session

The reset form previously accepted any authenticated session, including a pre-existing account preserved after a rejected recovery link. Auth state now records recovery owner and auth revision only after a successful recovery exchange and publication; each new link clears the previous grant, as do coordinated sign-in/register/sign-out/delete actions. The reset form and completion callbacks require matching recovery authority. Missing, wrong-owner and stale grants show the existing invalid-link view. PKCE reset-route codes without a type parameter are classified as recovery after their code exchange. Eight added tests cover both rejected token/code links preserving the existing account without recovery authority, successful implicit/PKCE exchanges (including missing type), and rendered invalid-grant denial. Full Jest: 1,438 tests / 124 suites pass; TypeScript passes; targeted lint has zero errors and 43 warnings in the existing auth files. Normal Android release compilation and iOS Hermes export succeed, with exact current auth/reset/link-helper sources verified in their maps. This is offline/mock verification; Resend SMTP 550 still blocks production email delivery and native recovery-link validation. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/recovery-session-authority/`.

## Conservative budget feasibility gate — 19 October 2026

The deployed function now rejects a proven unaffordable required-slot pool before provider configuration lookup or an AI call. It sums the cheapest known same-currency cost per required slot, including outerwear when layering is required. Owned pieces cost zero. Missing slots, unknown/invalid prices or currencies, and alternative dress compositions leave the result undecided. A lower bound above the cap proves impossibility; a lower bound below it does not prove style/compatibility feasibility and does not select or fabricate any outfit.

The new actual-handler regression failed before and passes after: three100USD required pieces under a250USD cap return422 `no_outfit_within_budget`, minimum300USD, without a provider request. Unknown prices and dress alternatives do not trigger a false rejection. All1,520 tests/132 suites,12 staged wire tests and staged Deno pass; scoped deployment succeeded.

Eight corrected production preflights pass. The first added-case run lacked weather and therefore required an absent outerwear slot; that fixture left the bound undecided and the case failed with provider503. The failed evidence is preserved in `budget-feasibility-preflight-missing-weather.json`; corrected fixtures specify20C. A separate real gym-catalog call returned422 in3.4 seconds with minimum4,043,000UZS above3,150,000UZS, before the model call. All three disposable fixtures were deleted and independent SQL found zero remaining tagged users in the run window. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/outfit-budget-feasibility-gate.json`, `production-budget-preflight.json`, `production-gym-budget-preflight.json`. The main mobile screen's generic error display still needs structured budget-code mapping; this does not make unaffordable inventory produce a valid outfit or certify10,000DAU.

## Bounded scheduled usage retention — 19 October 2026

Production inspection found zero usage-counter rows, no pg_cron installation/job, and no global day index. The bounded_usage_retention migration is deployed: a day index supports expiration lookup; prune_usage_counters deletes at most10,000rows per invocation with SKIP LOCKED; the existing cutoff retains today and the previous two UTC days. A named pg_cron job runs once per minute with a10-second statement deadline and removes only its own completed run-history records older than7days. Ordinary clients cannot execute pruning. ### Camera and onboarding flow repair — 2026-10-19

Found a reachable stale path: Paywall activation for fewer than three garments entered ScreenWardrobe, which posted to the older Render `/scan-wardrobe` route without its required bearer header and led to ReviewScan invoking absent `add-clothing-batch`. The activation now opens the current Camera; the existing ScanWardrobe route also maps to Camera, preserving route recognition and photo/video scanning through WardrobeVideo/useVideoAnalysis. Legacy files are retained, not deployed as working services.

Camera photo/video handlers previously navigated only when gallery permission was granted and gallery saving succeeded. Both now send captured local files to analysis independently of optional gallery saving. Microphone denial continues to stop recording and resets recording state. Twelve tests execute extracted production handlers and activation logic: unchanged baseline fails six cases; current passes all twelve. Full Jest 1,707 tests / 142 suites and TypeScript pass. iOS Hermes export and Android arm64 Release build succeeded; source maps match CameraScreen, PaywallScreen and RootNavigator exactly. Physical-device capture, real store transactions and complete native UI flows remain unverified. See `readiness-evidence/camera-flow-hardening.json` and preserved logs. Goal remains not certified for 10,000 DAU.


### Scan result truthfulness and review metadata — 2026-10-19

The shipped useVideoAnalysis hook was converting provider errors/unsuccessful classification and missing video frames into invented Clothing Item detections. These fabricated success paths are removed: failed analyses leave results null and display an error/processing notice. Successful classification now preserves actual color and known material instead of Unknown/grey, maps backend section names to supported display positions and rejects unknown sections. WardrobeVideo review now uses the shared category mapper (jacket opens outerwear) and passes material/style/confidence/description to the editor.

Verification: 11 actual React hook regressions and 3 actual extracted review-handler cases pass; full Jest 1,719 tests / 143 suites, TypeScript, iOS Hermes export and Android arm64 Release build pass. Both source maps exactly match the two changed runtime files. No live provider request was added. Remaining scope explicitly includes video first-frame-only aggregation, first-item review and editor persistence pattern/unknown-color audit; these changes do not prove complete garment detection, image accuracy or 10,000 DAU capacity. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/scan-result-truthfulness.json`.


### Clothing editor metadata preservation — 2026-10-19

New-item editor saves previously replaced every pattern with solid, collapsed specific types to the selected broad category and discarded detection confidence. The save now preserves a known pattern (empty string for unknown), compatible specific type and finite confidence in [0,1], including zero. User-selected category corrections supersede conflicting detected types. Row reload no longer fabricates solid for an absent pattern. Existing-item confidence initialization uses nullish coalescing rather than discarding zero.

Fourteen tests execute actual extracted save/reload handlers; full Jest 1,733 tests / 144 suites, TypeScript, iOS Hermes export and Android arm64 Release pass. Both source maps match the changed runtime files. A read-only production information_schema query confirmed pattern/sub_category text and detection_confidence double precision fields are nullable. Existing pattern database default solid remains, so omitted-field callers still need audit; no schema or live customer rows changed. No live write/read fixture or installed-device certification added. Unknown-color/season defaults, direct-editor analysis metadata and complete video multi-item review remain open. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/editor-metadata-preservation.json`.


### Direct-photo editor analysis and save race — 2026-10-19

The editor used the deprecated expo-file-system read API and retained only visible type/color/season values from direct analysis; material, confidence, description and specific garment identity were lost before save. It now imports expo-file-system/legacy, stores the classification result for metadata resolution and shows the existing localized failure notice if analysis fails. File/provider completions are guarded by auth revision and effect cleanup, with no one-shot ref that would suppress a replacement Strict Mode effect.

A rendered-screen test exposed that Save remained enabled while classification was pending. The button and handler now block this race; the completed result survives actual editor render and reaches the mocked store save payload. Seven extracted production handler/metadata cases and three new rendered direct-photo cases pass (eight cases in the editor screen suite). Full Jest 1,743 tests / 145 suites, TypeScript, iOS Hermes export and Android arm64 Release pass; source maps exactly match the current editor. Tests mock service/file/provider boundaries; there was no new live model call or physical-device verification. Unknown color/season defaults, multi-item video review, provider capacity and complete 10k DAU certification remain open. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/editor-direct-photo-analysis.json`.


### Client scan request and account isolation — 2026-10-19

useVideoAnalysis previously let overlapping requests overwrite results and loading state, and allowed completions after reset, logout/account change or unmount to publish/queue old photos. Request versions, auth revisions and mounted guards now gate file/frame extraction, provider dispatch/completion, result publication, progress, alerts and offline queue calls. Account changes clear already completed private results; cleanup removes listeners and invalidates work. Already dispatched provider requests are not physically aborted.

Eight actual React hook cases with controlled deferred file/provider operations verify latest-result wins, old completion cannot clear the new spinner, reset/account/unmount suppress stale network errors and queue writes, completed detections clear on account change, and native file/frame work stops before later provider calls after reset. ### Offline scan queue ownership audit — 2026-10-19

Queue entries had no owner and the inactive worker deleted entries whenever a void analysis Promise resolved, even when analysis failed. Store entries now carry authenticated ownerId; signed-out additions reject, and mutation/count APIs filter by current owner. The hook only acknowledges queue acceptance when addUpload returns true. Worker filtering now rejects foreign/unowned entries and attempts>=3, binds hooks to their owner, requires explicit true before removal, guards auth revision/lifecycle, serializes before awaiting connectivity and retains entries/releases lock on check failure. Raw private URI logging removed.

Five store and ten worker tests pass, including foreign-owner mutation rejection, signed-out additions, explicit vs unconfirmed results, exhausted retries, duplicate checks, stale hook/account/destruction and connectivity failure recovery. Full Jest 1,763 tests / 147 suites, TypeScript and iOS/Android release builds pass; source maps match reachable store/hook code. Worker has no active screen integration and is absent from both bundles. Current hook returns void, so explicit result protocol, active retry integration, persisted restart recovery and user review remain required. Queue acceptance is not a disk-flush guarantee. No live scan or provider capacity retest occurred. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/offline-scan-owner-isolation.json`.


### Offline scan worker connected to review — 2026-10-19

Superseding the prior inactive-worker evidence, WardrobeVideo now binds/initializes the retry worker and destroys it on cleanup. A gate excludes initial media, current analysis and existing results, and each worker pass stops after one explicit successful publication so another queued scan cannot overwrite a pending review. Scan methods return true only after valid current results are published; failure/cancellation returns false. Retry options suppress duplicate enqueue and modal failure alerts. Transport/timeout categories survive the ExternalAIService boundary; provider rejection remains distinct. Normal offline acknowledgement waits for queue persistence flush and a current-identity check, with storage-failure regression preventing false acknowledgement. Worker recovers owner-bound interrupted processing entries on initialization/destruction, and explicit unreachable internet does not consume attempts.

Four integration cases execute the actual extracted screen binding with the real hook/worker/store: one successful review plus next-entry preservation, failure retention/no duplicate/no modal, flushed owner-tagged offline acknowledgement, and storage-failure rejection. Thirteen worker cases and three new transport classification cases pass. Full Jest 1,773 tests / 148 suites, TypeScript and both release builds pass. All four changed runtime files match both source maps; worker is now included in both release bundles.

No device, OS-background or live provider behavior is certified. Worker runs only while the review screen is mounted. Queued native URIs still require durable media copying/cleanup, legacy unowned handling, restart verification and exhausted-entry UI/manual retry. Earlier NVIDIA concurrency failure remains open; no extra live provider call was made. Evidence: `readiness-evidence/offline-scan-integration.json`.


### Durable queued scan media — 2026-10-19

Native offline retry now stages a private owner-specific Documents copy before adding/flush-acknowledging its queue record. File size is bounded before copy (14MiB image/100MiB video), copy and final commit sizes are checked, destination collisions reject, and partial/account-changed commits clean up. The worker resolves managed URIs against the current container path, persists successful queue removal before deleting only managed owner files, and account cleanup independently removes staged scan media through the same serialized file-work gate. Original picker/camera sources are never deleted by managed cleanup. Expo SDK57 legacy filesystem documentation was checked: https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/sdk/filesystem-legacy/ .

The actual staging module ran through a Node filesystem adapter, followed by a separate child-process restart after original inputs were removed and Documents moved to a different container.666,026-byte real JPEG and1,048,576-byte synthetic video-copy payload retained exact SHA256 hashes. Wrong-owner resolution/deletion was blocked; managed deletion passed; partial-copy and account-switch-during-copy rejected without publishing extra files; sparse>100MiB input rejected before any copy. The synthetic payload does not establish video validity/decoding. A native-branch hook regression proves persisted queue URI uses the staged path rather than temporary capture. Full Jest 1,774 tests / 148 suites, TypeScript and both release builds pass with exact four-file source-map matches.

This is not native Expo filesystem or full app restart certification. Queue hydration/restart, real video decode, legacy unowned entries, exhausted-entry/manual retry UI, native content-URI behavior and orphan reconciliation across interrupted file/record commits remain open. ### 20 October 2026: native scan review durability

Native scan review durability: completed results are committed before success is published; saved candidates are acknowledged after wardrobe persistence. Fresh OS-process tests also cover partial review completion, account isolation and source completion markers; editor ordering, repeat-save deduplication, screen restore and offline queue cleanup have regression coverage. These probes use generated media and a synthetic cache owner, not a complete authenticated shipping-app flow. Web durability, large-payload/storage-failure and power-loss coverage, corrupt-record recovery and unmarked capture recovery remain unfinished. Evidence: [durable scan reviews](readiness-evidence/durable-scan-reviews.json).

### 20 October 2026: scan review cleanup hardening
Scan review cleanup hardening: empty/partial acknowledgement files no longer hide unsaved candidates. Completed reviews retry image cleanup on refresh, and the source-completion marker is read back before review deletion. Four injected-failure regression cases cover empty acknowledgements, deletion failure/retry, invalid completion-marker writes and continued access to another pending review during cleanup failure. Full Jest **1,856 / 153 suites**, TypeScript, iOS Hermes and Android arm64 Release pass; changed service matches both source maps. Fresh OS-process restart probe passes again. Earlier native restart probes predate this cleanup change; complete native disk-failure/power-loss and corrupt-record recovery remain open. Evidence: [review cleanup hardening](readiness-evidence/scan-review-cleanup.json).

### 20 October 2026: scan review validation

Scan review validation: duplicate candidate IDs, invalid timestamps and invalid source IDs are rejected before any saved-item acknowledgement. Review ID collisions cannot overwrite committed data, and candidate-ID collisions cannot publish a result. Five new regression cases pass. The fresh-process OS-filesystem probe additionally confirms damaged records remain intact while valid records list, and duplicate IDs cannot produce acknowledgement files. Full Jest **1,866 tests / 153 suites**, TypeScript, iOS Hermes and Android arm64 Release pass; changed runtime service matches both maps. This is local integrity/recovery evidence, not provider output accuracy or a native power-loss test.

### 20 October 2026: bounded direct NVIDIA diagnosis

Latest direct NVIDIA diagnosis (20 October): three sequential calls through the exact current classifier used byte-identical JPEG input (original fixture and native-path file hashes match). Two returned beige jacket in **5.202s** and **44.595s**; the third timed out at **45.006s**. Raw provider output already contained the accepted jacket category, confirming no category conversion on these responses. This does not explain away the earlier jeans prediction: visual misclassification remains open. The new run confirms provider latency/reliability failure even without concurrent load; no production configuration was changed and no capacity readiness is claimed. Diagnostic script accepts the existing provider credential over non-echoing stdin, never writes it, and returns a failing status when the full primary-match check fails. Evidence: [current direct provider diagnosis](readiness-evidence/nvidia-primary-garment-current.json).

### 20 October 2026: complete client scan deadlines

Complete client scan deadlines: SDK invocation is bounded even if the network/auth/body promise never settles. Classify calls allow60s; one-to-three-frame batches allow60/105/150s to cover sequential45s server calls plus overhead; legacy multi-operation processing allows180s. Deadline failure aborts the client transport, returns timeout without a fabricated classification, clears timers and cannot be replaced by late success. Four new regression cases pass; actual installed Supabase FunctionsClient integration also passes controlled fetch/body stalls with the production wrapper and a controlled clock. Full suite **1,870 tests / 153 suites**, TypeScript and both release builds pass; changed service matches both source maps. This does not cancel already-dispatched provider work on the server, reduce NVIDIA latency, prove native UI behavior, or establish capacity. Evidence: [SDK deadline integration](readiness-evidence/scan-client-sdk-deadline.json).

### 20 October 2026: remove unnecessary photo scan operations

Photo scan processing repair: wardrobe image analysis now calls `classifyOnly` instead of legacy `processClothingImage`/`all`. Classification retains the original image and avoids optional angle normalization, background removal, detection and enhancement paths that could add provider requests/cost/latency unrelated to adding a garment. The photo request now uses the60s classification deadline. An integration test runs the production hook through the actual service wrapper and verifies one `classify` invocation, original-image conservation and classification-only provenance. Existing offline/retry/ownership tests pass using the new path. Full suite **1,871 tests / 153 suites**, TypeScript, iOS Hermes and Android arm64 Release pass; changed hook matches both maps. No backend configuration changed. Installed native UI, actual latency savings and complete provider accuracy/capacity remain unverified.

### 20 October 2026: saved-scan outage retry control

Saved-scan outage retry control: automatic failures now persist retry deadlines (30s,120s, then300s) and stop after one failure per pass. A cooldown applies across the current owner's captures, preventing repeated network/screen checks from moving immediately to the next capture during an outage. Explicit Retry clears the selected entry's cooldown and can run immediately; account isolation and the three-attempt ceiling remain. Already-durable review/source cleanup still bypasses cooldown, including offline/exhausted entries. Four new regression cases verify persisted deadlines, processor restart and repeated checks, queue-wide failure restraint, manual retry and owner isolation; existing exhausted-source cleanup test also covers future cooldown. Full suite **1,875 tests / 153 suites**, TypeScript and both release builds pass; both runtime files match both maps. This is controlled queue/service coverage, not a fresh native process/clock-change/power-loss test or provider-wide capacity control. NVIDIA accuracy and measured peak capacity remain unresolved.

### 20 October 2026: serializer code-injection patch

Serializer code-injection patch: `serialize-javascript`6.0.2→7.1.2 is pinned by exact override.
